{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0118Z3GrTsbH7vhuQsB5H5mt","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Validate tool input against the registered inputSchema\n2\t\n3\tIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#37\n4\tLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:M, area:tools, epic:extension-points\n5\t\n6\tPart of #35\n7\t\n8\t## Why\n9\t\n10\t`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\n11\twithout ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\n12\tEvery tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\n13\tproviders, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The shipped example shows the\n14\tshape of the problem: `example/weather-tool.ts` declares `location` as a required string, then\n15\tcasts and defaults it (`(input.location as string) || 'Unknown'`) with a comment warning the reader\n16\tthat `inputSchema` shapes what the model sends but does not enforce it. That is the careful version,\n17\twritten by the people who wrote the engine — and it still answers a malformed call with a\n18\tconfidently formatted forecast for `'Unknown'` rather than telling the model it got the call wrong.\n19\t\n20\tThe registry currently pushes this check onto each tool's own executor, leaving every consumer\n21\tto implement it itself — differently each time, or not at all. A tool that receives unexpected input must fail\n22\tin one predictable, known way before the rest of this epic's tool work can be built on top\n23\tof it.\n24\t\n25\t## What to do\n26\t\n27\tValidate `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` inside\n28\t`ToolRegistry.execute`, before the executor runs, using a JSON Schema validator (`ajv`\n29\tunless the build proves otherwise) added to `dependencies` — not a second, hand-written\n30\tschema in a different format, since the schema already has to match what is handed to the\n31\tproviders.\n32\t\n33\tCompile a static tool's schema once at `register` time so a broken schema fails at startup,\n34\tnot mid-conversation; a schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource`\n35\tresolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object.\n36\t\n37\tA rejection returns a `ToolResponse` (`{result: '<what was wrong>'}`) rather than throwing,\n38\tso a malformed call becomes something the model can read and correct within the existing\n39\t`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` budget, instead of ending the whole conversation with a\n40\tgeneric transport error. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only —\n41\tit does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and\n42\tthere is no opt-out flag.\n43\t\n44\tUpdate `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md` and `.specify/spec.md` to match.\n45\t\n46\t## Acceptance criteria\n47\t\n48\t- [ ] `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's\n49\t      `inputSchema` before calling the executor; on failure it returns a `ToolResponse`\n50\t      whose `result` describes the failure, and the executor is never invoked\n51\t- [ ] A new `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` (the registry has no test file today)\n52\t      registers a tool requiring a `location` string, calls `execute` with `{}`, and\n53\t      asserts the returned result names `location` as required and the executor's call\n54\t      count is `0`\n55\t- [ ] A test asserts valid input with an unlisted extra property is passed through to the\n56\t      executor unchanged (`toEqual`, so an added default or coerced value fails the test) —\n57\t      exactly once\n58\t- [ ] A test asserts a wrong-typed field (a number where a string is required) is rejected\n59\t      with a message naming the field path and the expected type, and that the message does\n60\t      **not** contain the offending value\n61\t- [ ] A test asserts `register` throws at registration time for an uncompilable schema, and\n62\t      that the thrown message names the tool\n63\t- [ ] A test asserts a function-valued tool definition whose returned schema differs between\n64\t      calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one\n65\t- [ ] A test asserts a static tool's schema is compiled once across ten `execute` calls\n66\t- [ ] A tool-loop test in `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` drives a stub provider through a\n67\t      rejected tool call followed by a corrected retry, and asserts `processMessage`\n68\t      resolves — not rejects — with the rejection text visible to the provider on the next\n69\t      round\n70\t- [ ] `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged,\n71\t      pinned by a test\n72\t- [ ] Rejection messages and log lines name the failing property paths and the constraint\n73\t      that failed, and never a property **value**. Tool input is where identifying data\n74\t      enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing today. A test asserts a rejected\n75\t      payload containing an identifying field produces no log line and no returned text\n76\t      containing that field's value\n77\t- [ ] `execute` carries a **one-line** doc comment — `re-lint/max-comment-lines` rejects a\n78\t      `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — and the full contract (rejection `ToolResponse` for input\n79\t      the schema rejects, throw only for an unknown tool name) is stated in\n80\t      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, which the comment points at\n81\t- [ ] The validator is in `package.json` `dependencies`, not `peerDependencies` or\n82\t      `devDependencies`, and\n83\t      `npm run build && node --input-type=module -e \"import('./dist/index.js')\"` exits `0`\n84\t- [ ] `README.md` states what the registry now does, and the claim is true rather than\n85\t      merely present\n86\t- [ ] `docs/adding-a-tool.md` no longer tells executors to cast defensively and handle\n87\t      missing input; it states that input reaching an executor has already passed the\n88\t      declared schema, that unknown keys may still be present, and that a rejection is\n89\t      returned to the model rather than thrown\n90\t- [ ] `.specify/spec.md` records that a rejection is a tool result (not an exception), that\n91\t      the tool-round bound is what stops a retry cycle from spinning, and that extra\n92\t      properties pass through unmodified\n93\t- [ ] The PR body states the migration note: an executor that used to hand-validate its\n94\t      input now receives only input the schema already accepts, and a tool that relied on\n95\t      seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it. The version\n96\t      bump is MINOR\n97\t- [ ] Every test this issue adds cites the statement it validates with an inline\n98\t      `([validated by ...])` link in a `specs/**` or `adrs/**` file, and the statements exist\n99\t      before the tests do. `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails the build on an uncited test, so the\n100\t      spec edits are part of this issue rather than a follow-up\n101\t- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry for this change, written for somebody reading it on npm\n102\t      rather than somebody reading this repo's log. CI runs `scripts/check-changelog.sh` and\n103\t      fails any PR touching a non-test file under `src/` without one\n104\t- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`)\n105\t      and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the changed public surface\n106\t\n107\t## Tech notes\n108\t\n109\tToday's `throw` in `registry.ts` propagates through the orchestrator's tool-call\n110\t`Promise.all`, out of the processing loop's `try`, fires `onError`, and reaches the\n111\tWebSocket error handler, which sends a generic \"Failed to process message\" and never sends\n112\tthe stream-end frame. That end-to-end path is why a rejection has to be a returned\n113\t`ToolResponse` instead of a throw.\n114\t\n115\tDeliberately out of scope: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is — the same failure\n116\tclass handled the opposite way, which is an intentional split, but changing it affects\n117\tdirect callers and is tracked as *Return an unknown tool name to the model instead of\n118\tthrowing*. Also out of scope: validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an\n119\tevaluation harness; per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still\n120\tvalid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern that no issue in this epic\n121\towns (*Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook* is a veto seam, not an\n122\tauthorization model); retry, timeout\n123\tand rate limiting around tool execution; and any actual tool implementation, which is\n124\twritten against this contract later.\n125\t\n126\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"a60c76d3-e266-4b4d-b41f-92aec5eb9847","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:39:01.379Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/ff5ba54c6ad0/workspace/ticket.md","content":"# Validate tool input against the registered inputSchema\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#37\nLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:M, area:tools, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up, logs the input's key names, and calls the executor\nwithout ever checking the declared `inputSchema` (`src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts`).\nEvery tool declares a JSON Schema (`src/types/ai.ts`) that is handed verbatim to both\nproviders, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The shipped example shows the\nshape of the problem: `example/weather-tool.ts` declares `location` as a required string, then\ncasts and defaults it (`(input.location as string) || 'Unknown'`) with a comment warning the reader\nthat `inputSchema` shapes what the model sends but does not enforce it. That is the careful version,\nwritten by the people who wrote the engine — and it still answers a malformed call with a\nconfidently formatted forecast for `'Unknown'` rather than telling the model it got the call wrong.\n\nThe registry currently pushes this check onto each tool's own executor, leaving every consumer\nto implement it itself — differently each time, or not at all. A tool that receives unexpected input must fail\nin one predictable, known way before the rest of this epic's tool work can be built on top\nof it.\n\n## What to do\n\nValidate `input` against the resolved tool definition's `inputSchema` inside\n`ToolRegistry.execute`, before the executor runs, using a JSON Schema validator (`ajv`\nunless the build proves otherwise) added to `dependencies` — not a second, hand-written\nschema in a different format, since the schema already has to match what is handed to the\nproviders.\n\nCompile a static tool's schema once at `register` time so a broken schema fails at startup,\nnot mid-conversation; a schema produced by a function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource`\nresolves and compiles per call, cached by the resolved schema object.\n\nA rejection returns a `ToolResponse` (`{result: '<what was wrong>'}`) rather than throwing,\nso a malformed call becomes something the model can read and correct within the existing\n`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5` budget, instead of ending the whole conversation with a\ngeneric transport error. Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only —\nit does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults, and\nthere is no opt-out flag.\n\nUpdate `docs/adding-a-tool.md`, `README.md` and `.specify/spec.md` to match.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's\n      `inputSchema` before calling the executor; on failure it returns a `ToolResponse`\n      whose `result` describes the failure, and the executor is never invoked\n- [ ] A new `src/orchestration/tools/registry.test.ts` (the registry has no test file today)\n      registers a tool requiring a `location` string, calls `execute` with `{}`, and\n      asserts the returned result names `location` as required and the executor's call\n      count is `0`\n- [ ] A test asserts valid input with an unlisted extra property is passed through to the\n      executor unchanged (`toEqual`, so an added default or coerced value fails the test) —\n      exactly once\n- [ ] A test asserts a wrong-typed field (a number where a string is required) is rejected\n      with a message naming the field path and the expected type, and that the message does\n      **not** contain the offending value\n- [ ] A test asserts `register` throws at registration time for an uncompilable schema, and\n      that the thrown message names the tool\n- [ ] A test asserts a function-valued tool definition whose returned schema differs between\n      calls is validated against the schema current at that call, not a stale cached one\n- [ ] A test asserts a static tool's schema is compiled once across ten `execute` calls\n- [ ] A tool-loop test in `chatOrchestrator.test.ts` drives a stub provider through a\n      rejected tool call followed by a corrected retry, and asserts `processMessage`\n      resolves — not rejects — with the rejection text visible to the provider on the next\n      round\n- [ ] `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name, unchanged,\n      pinned by a test\n- [ ] Rejection messages and log lines name the failing property paths and the constraint\n      that failed, and never a property **value**. Tool input is where identifying data\n      enters the engine and the engine redacts nothing today. A test asserts a rejected\n      payload containing an identifying field produces no log line and no returned text\n      containing that field's value\n- [ ] `execute` carries a **one-line** doc comment — `re-lint/max-comment-lines` rejects a\n      `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` block — and the full contract (rejection `ToolResponse` for input\n      the schema rejects, throw only for an unknown tool name) is stated in\n      `specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md`, which the comment points at\n- [ ] The validator is in `package.json` `dependencies`, not `peerDependencies` or\n      `devDependencies`, and\n      `npm run build && node --input-type=module -e \"import('./dist/index.js')\"` exits `0`\n- [ ] `README.md` states what the registry now does, and the claim is true rather than\n      merely present\n- [ ] `docs/adding-a-tool.md` no longer tells executors to cast defensively and handle\n      missing input; it states that input reaching an executor has already passed the\n      declared schema, that unknown keys may still be present, and that a rejection is\n      returned to the model rather than thrown\n- [ ] `.specify/spec.md` records that a rejection is a tool result (not an exception), that\n      the tool-round bound is what stops a retry cycle from spinning, and that extra\n      properties pass through unmodified\n- [ ] The PR body states the migration note: an executor that used to hand-validate its\n      input now receives only input the schema already accepts, and a tool that relied on\n      seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it. The version\n      bump is MINOR\n- [ ] Every test this issue adds cites the statement it validates with an inline\n      `([validated by ...])` link in a `specs/**` or `adrs/**` file, and the statements exist\n      before the tests do. `re-lint/require-spec-link` fails the build on an uncited test, so the\n      spec edits are part of this issue rather than a follow-up\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry for this change, written for somebody reading it on npm\n      rather than somebody reading this repo's log. CI runs `scripts/check-changelog.sh` and\n      fails any PR touching a non-test file under `src/` without one\n- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`)\n      and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the changed public surface\n\n## Tech notes\n\nToday's `throw` in `registry.ts` propagates through the orchestrator's tool-call\n`Promise.all`, out of the processing loop's `try`, fires `onError`, and reaches the\nWebSocket error handler, which sends a generic \"Failed to process message\" and never sends\nthe stream-end frame. That end-to-end path is why a rejection has to be a returned\n`ToolResponse` instead of a throw.\n\nDeliberately out of scope: the `Unknown tool: <name>` throw stays as-is — the same failure\nclass handled the opposite way, which is an intentional split, but changing it affects\ndirect callers and is tracked as *Return an unknown tool name to the model instead of\nthrowing*. Also out of scope: validating tool *output*; hallucination detection and an\nevaluation harness; per-tool authorization — a schema-valid call for someone else's data is still\nvalid, and authorization is a separate `ToolContext` boundary concern that no issue in this epic\nowns (*Let a consumer decline a tool call through a `beforeToolCall` hook* is a veto seam, not an\nauthorization model); retry, timeout\nand rate limiting around tool execution; and any actual tool implementation, which is\nwritten against this contract later.\n\n","numLines":126,"startLine":1,"totalLines":126}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_0118Z3GrTsbH7vhuQsB5H5mt","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01BbxeJmVqSWetd7L1p3493Q","type":"tool_result","content":"import type {ToolDefinition} from '../../types/ai.js';\nimport type {OutgoingMessage} from '../../types/messages.js';\nimport {log} from '../../shared/logger.js';\n\nexport interface ToolResponse {\n  result: string;\n  clientMessages?: OutgoingMessage[];\n  suppressAssistantResponse?: boolean;\n}\n\nexport interface ToolContext {\n  userId: string | number;\n  sessionId?: string;\n  workspaceId?: string | number;\n  authHeaders?: {\n    cookie?: string;\n    authorization?: string;\n    host?: string;\n  };\n}\n\nexport type ToolExecutor = (input: Record<string, unknown>, context?: ToolContext) => Promise<string | ToolResponse>;\n\nexport function normalizeToolResponse(response: string | ToolResponse): ToolResponse {\n  return typeof response === 'string' ? {result: response} : response;\n}\n\nexport type ToolDefinitionSource = ToolDefinition | (() => ToolDefinition);\n\nexport interface RegisteredTool {\n  definition: ToolDefinition;\n  execute: ToolExecutor;\n}\n\ninterface StoredTool {\n  definitionSource: ToolDefinitionSource;\n  execute: ToolExecutor;\n}\n\nexport class ToolRegistry {\n  private readonly tools = new Map<string, StoredTool>();\n\n  private resolveDefinition(source: ToolDefinitionSource): ToolDefinition {\n    return typeof source === 'function' ? source() : source;\n  }\n\n  register(definition: ToolDefinitionSource, execute: ToolExecutor): void {\n    const resolved = this.resolveDefinition(definition);\n    this.tools.set(resolved.name, {definitionSource: definition, execute});\n  }\n\n  get(name: string): RegisteredTool | undefined {\n    const tool = this.tools.get(name);\n    if (!tool) return undefined;\n    return {definition: this.resolveDefinition(tool.definitionSource), execute: tool.execute};\n  }\n\n  getDefinitions(): ToolDefinition[] {\n    return [...this.tools.values()].map(t => this.resolveDefinition(t.definitionSource));\n  }\n\n  getPromptInstructions(): string[] {\n    return this.getDefinitions()\n      .filter(t => t.promptInstructions)\n      .map(t => `${t.name}: ${t.promptInstructions}`);\n  }\n\n  getExamplePrompts(): string[] {\n    return this.getDefinitions().flatMap(t => t.examplePrompts ?? []);\n  }\n\n  has(name: string): boolean {\n    return this.tools.has(name);\n  }\n\n  async execute(name: string, input: Record<string, unknown>, context?: ToolContext): Promise<ToolResponse> {\n    const tool = this.tools.get(name);\n    if (!tool) {\n      log.warn('tool', 'tool not found', {name});\n      throw new Error(`Unknown tool: ${name}`);\n    }\n    log.info('tool', 'executing', {name, inputKeys: Object.keys(input)});\n    const rawResult = await tool.execute(input, context);\n    const response = normalizeToolResponse(rawResult);\n    log.info('tool', 'execution complete', {\n      name,\n      rawType: typeof rawResult,\n      clientMessageCount: response.clientMessages?.length ?? 0,\n      clientMessageTypes: response.clientMessages?.map(m => m.type) ?? [],\n      suppressAssistantResponse: response.suppressAssistantResponse ?? false,\n    });\n    return response;\n  }\n}","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"5d6d5e54-14de-406c-b361-eef07e353b71","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:39:05.781Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"import type {ToolDefinition} from '../../types/ai.js';\nimport type {OutgoingMessage} from '../../types/messages.js';\nimport {log} from '../../shared/logger.js';\n\nexport interface ToolResponse {\n  result: string;\n  clientMessages?: OutgoingMessage[];\n  suppressAssistantResponse?: boolean;\n}\n\nexport interface ToolContext {\n  userId: string | number;\n  sessionId?: string;\n  workspaceId?: string | number;\n  authHeaders?: {\n    cookie?: string;\n    authorization?: string;\n    host?: string;\n  };\n}\n\nexport type ToolExecutor = (input: Record<string, unknown>, context?: ToolContext) => Promise<string | ToolResponse>;\n\nexport function normalizeToolResponse(response: string | ToolResponse): ToolResponse {\n  return typeof response === 'string' ? []);\n  }\n\n  has(name: string): boolean {\n    return this.tools.has(name);\n  }\n\n  async execute(name: string, input: Record<string, unknown>, context?: ToolContext): Promise<ToolResponse> {\n    const tool = this.tools.get(name);\n    if (!tool) {\n      log.warn('tool', 'tool not found', {name});\n      throw new Error(`Unknown tool: ${name}`);\n    }\n    log.info('tool', 'executing', {name, inputKeys: Object.keys(input)});\n    const rawResult = await tool.execute(input, context);\n    const response = normalizeToolResponse(rawResult);\n    log.info('tool', 'execution complete', {\n      name,\n      rawType: typeof rawResult,\n      clientMessageCount: response.clientMessages?.length ?? 0,\n      clientMessageTypes: response.clientMessages?.map(m => m.type) ?? {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014py8nUb3hKnkv95wRL2qkv","type":"tool_result","content":"spec.md\n# Tool Responses\n\n| Field  | Value |\n| ------ | ----- |\n| Issue  | n/a   |\n| Status | Draft |\n\nBy default, a tool executor returns a plain string that feeds back into the AI conversation. The client never sees the result -- it only sees the tool invocation (name and input parameters). This is fine for most tools, but sometimes a tool knows exactly what to show the user and does not need the AI to rephrase it.\n\nThe `ToolResponse` interface gives tools two additional capabilities:\n\n1. **Client messages** -- send one or more WebSocket messages directly to the client\n2. **Suppress the AI response** -- keep the model's reply in conversation context but hide it from the client\n\nThese are opt-in. Tools that return a plain string continue to work exactly as before.\n\n## The ToolResponse Interface\n\n<!-- doc-block: src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts#ToolResponse -->\n```typescript\ninterface ToolResponse {\n  result: string;\n  clientMessages?: OutgoingMessage[];\n  suppressAssistantResponse?: boolean;\n}\n```\n\n| Field | Purpose |\n|-------|---------|\n| `result` | The string the AI model sees -- identical to what a plain-string executor returns |\n| `clientMessages` | Optional array of `OutgoingMessage` values sent directly to the client via WebSocket |\n| `suppressAssistantResponse` | When `true`, the AI's next text response still accumulates in `session.entries` for context continuity, but is not sent to the client |\n\nA `ToolExecutor` can return either a plain `string` or a `ToolResponse`:\n\n<!-- doc-block: src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts#ToolExecutor -->\n```typescript\ntype ToolExecutor = (input: Record<string, unknown>, context?: ToolContext) => Promise<string | ToolResponse>;\n```\n\n`context` carries the session the call belongs to -- `userId`, `sessionId`, `workspaceId`, and the `authHeaders` forwarded from the WebSocket upgrade, so a tool can proxy the caller's credentials upstream.\n\nThe registry normalizes both forms internally via `normalizeToolResponse()`, so downstream code always sees a `ToolResponse`.\n\n## When to Use This\n\n**Client messages** make sense when the tool result is self-explanatory and the AI does not need to interpret it for the user. A unit conversion, a status update, a formatted data table -- these are cases where the tool knows the best presentation and the AI would just parrot the same information.\n\n**Suppression** pairs naturally with client messages. If the tool already sent the answer to the client, the AI's follow-up (\"The conversion result is 160.93 km\") adds no value. Suppression hides that echo while keeping it in the conversation so the AI remembers the context for follow-up questions.\n\nUse plain string returns (the default) when the AI needs to interpret or summarize the tool result for the user. Database lookups and search results are good examples -- the raw data benefits from the AI explaining it in context.\n\n## Data Flow\n\n```\nPlain string:    executor returns \"data\"\n                   -> normalizeToolResponse wraps to {result: \"data\"}\n                   -> orchestrator feeds result to AI\n                   -> AI responds, client sees the response\n\nWith client messages:\n                 executor returns {result, clientMessages, suppressAssistantResponse}\n                   -> orchestrator extracts .result for AI (unchanged)\n                   -> orchestrator yields tool_result chunk carrying clientMessages\n                   -> if suppress, orchestrator yields suppress_output chunk\n                   -> messageHandler sends clientMessages to client\n                   -> messageHandler blanks pre-tool assistant entries (entry_upsert with empty content)\n                   -> messageHandler skips WebSocket sends for suppressed entries\n                   -> messageHandler sends entry_skip for each new suppressed entry\n                   -> suppressed entries still accumulate in session.entries\n                   -> client adjusts incoming indices using tracked skip list\n```\n\nThe key insight: suppressed entries are invisible to the client but visible to the AI. The conversation context stays intact, so the model can reference previous tool results even when the user did not see the AI's commentary on them.\n\n## Example: Unit Converter\n\nThe `convert_units` tool demonstrates all three features: client messages, suppression, and index assignment. A conversion like \"100 miles to kilometers\" has a definitive answer -- the AI does not need to elaborate.\n\n<!-- doc-block: none -- a simplified tool, marked simplified in its own first line -->","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"da21f98c-e5cf-4558-afbd-e3d000b1817c","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:39:09.376Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"spec.md\n# Tool Responses\n\n| Field  | Value |\n| ------ | ----- |\n| Issue  | n/a   |\n| Status | Draft |\n\nBy default, a tool executor returns a plain string that feeds back into the AI conversation. The client never sees the result -- it only sees the tool invocation (name and input parameters). This is fine for most tools, but sometimes a tool knows exactly what to show the user and does not need the AI to rephrase it.\n\nThe `ToolResponse` interface gives tools two additional capabilities:\n\n1. **Client messages** -- send one or more WebSocket messages directly to the client\n2. **Suppress the AI response** -- keep the model's reply in conversation context but hide it from the client\n\nThese are opt-in. Tools that return a plain string continue to work exactly as before.\n\n## The ToolResponse Interface\n\n<!-- doc-block: src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts#ToolResponse -->\n```typescript\ninterface ToolResponse {\n  result: string;\n  clientMessages?: OutgoingMessage[];\n  suppressAssistantResponse?: boolean;\n}\n```\n\n| Field | Purpose |\n|-------|---------|\n| `result` | The string the AI model sees -- identical to what a plain-string executor returns |\n| `clientMessages` | Optional array of `OutgoingMessage` values sent directly to the client via WebSocket |\n| `suppressAssistantResponse` | When `true`, the AI's next text response still accumulates in `session.entries` for context continuity, but is not sent to the client |\n\nA `ToolExecutor` can return either a plain `string` or a `ToolResponse`:\n\n<!-- doc-block: src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts#ToolExecutor -->\n```typescript\ntype ToolExecutor = (input: Record<string, unknown>, context?: ToolContext) => Promise<string | ToolResponse>;\n```\n\n`context` carries the session the call belongs to -- `userId`, `sessionId`, `workspaceId`, and the `authHeaders` forwarded from the WebSocket upgrade, so a tool can proxy the caller's credentials upstream.\n\nThe registry normalizes both forms internally via `normalizeToolResponse()`, so downstream code always sees a `ToolResponse`.\n\n## When to Use This\n\n**Client messages** make sense when the tool result is self-explanatory and the AI does not need to interpret it for the user. A unit conversion, a status update, a formatted data table -- these are cases where the tool knows the best presentation and the AI would just parrot the same information.\n\n**Suppression** pairs naturally with client messages. If the tool already sent the answer to the client, the AI's follow-up (\"The conversion result is 160.93 km\") adds no value. Suppression hides that echo while keeping it in the conversation so the AI remembers the context for follow-up questions.\n\nUse plain string returns (the default) when the AI needs to interpret or summarize the tool result for the user. Database lookups and search results are good examples -- the raw data benefits from the AI explaining it in context.\n\n## Data Flow\n\n```\nPlain string:    executor returns \"data\"\n                   -> normalizeToolResponse wraps to {result: \"data\"}\n                   -> orchestrator feeds result to AI\n                   -> AI responds, client sees the response\n\nWith client messages:\n                 executor returns {result, clientMessages, suppressAssistantResponse}\n                   -> orchestrator extracts .result for AI (unchanged)\n                   -> orchestrator yields tool_result chunk carrying clientMessages\n                   -> if suppress, orchestrator yields suppress_output chunk\n                   -> messageHandler sends clientMessages to client\n                   -> messageHandler blanks pre-tool assistant entries (entry_upsert with empty content)\n                   -> messageHandler skips WebSocket sends for suppressed entries\n                   -> messageHandler sends entry_skip for each new suppressed entry\n                   -> suppressed entries still accumulate in session.entries\n                   -> client adjusts incoming indices using tracked skip list\n```\n\nThe key insight: suppressed entries are invisible to the client but visible to the AI. The conversation context stays intact, so the model can reference previous tool results even when the user did not see the AI's commentary on them.\n\n## Example: Unit Converter\n\nThe `convert_units` tool demonstrates all three features: client messages, suppression, and index assignment. A conversion like \"100 miles to kilometers\" has a definitive answer -- the AI does not need to elaborate.\n\n<!-- doc-block: none -- a simplified tool, marked simplified in its own first line -->","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_014py8nUb3hKnkv95wRL2qkv","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

provider: {\n      async *sendMessage(params: SendMessageParams): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n        sent.systemPrompt = params.systemPrompt;\n        const last = 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'};\n      },\n      async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n        return {} as T;\n      },\n    },\n  };\n};\n\ndescribe('ChatOrchestrator hooks', () => {\n orchestrator.processMessage(createSession('hello'));\n\n      expect(callOrder[0]).toBe('beforeSession');\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('afterSession', () => {\n    it('called after everything expect(callOrder).toEqual(['afterModelResponse', 'afterSession']);\n    });\n\n    it('called even when an error occurs', async () => {\n      let afterSessionCalled = false;\n      const hooks: OrchestratorHooks = {\n        onError: async () => {},\n        afterSession: async () => {\n          afterSessionCalled = true;\n        },\n      };\n\n      const orchestrator = outcomeOf(orchestrator.processMessage(createSession('hello')));\n\n      expect({outcome, afterSessionCalled}).toEqual({outcome: 'boom', afterSessionCalled: true});\n    });\n  });\n\n {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NwqCccgkdGFKr4Nc7uYVw5","type":"tool_result","content":"    const response = await toolRegistry.execute(tc.name, tc.input);\n    return {tc, response};\n  })\n);\n\n// Collect client messages from all tools\nconst clientMessages = responses.flatMap(({response}) => response.clientMessages ?? []);\n\n// Yield them as a chunk for the message handler\nif (clientMessages.length > 0) {\n  yield {type: 'tool_result', clientMessages};\n}\n\n// Signal suppression if any tool requested it\nif (responses.some(({response}) => response.suppressAssistantResponse)) {\n  yield {type: 'suppress_output'};\n}\n```\n\n### Message Handler\n\nTwo new chunk types flow through `processChunk`:\n\n- `tool_result` -- each client message in the array is serialized and sent directly to the WebSocket\n- `suppress_output` -- sets `state.suppressOutput = true` on the stream state\n\nWhen suppression is active, `streamSegment` and `commitAndClear` still call `appendEntry`, `appendDelta`, and `commitEntry` (so the session accumulates entries for context), but skip `sendUpsert`, `sendDelta`, and `sendCommit` (so the client sees nothing).\n\nWhen a new entry is created under suppression, the server sends an `entry_skip` message with the server-side index. This tells the client \"an entry exists here on the server, but you will never receive it.\" The client tracks these skipped indices and adjusts all subsequent incoming indices via the `adjustIndex` pure function. Without this, server indices would diverge from the client's entry array, causing deltas and commits to target the wrong positions.\n\nIf the AI streamed text *before* the tool call (which happens when the model explains what it is about to do), that text was already sent to the client as an assistant entry. When suppression activates, the server retroactively blanks these entries by sending `entry_upsert` with empty content. The client already skips rendering empty assistant entries, so the pre-tool text disappears cleanly.\n\n**Suppression is sticky for the entire stream.** Once a tool sets `suppressAssistantResponse: true` in any round, all subsequent AI responses in that stream are suppressed -- even if later tool rounds do not set the flag. This is intentional: suppression is a per-message property, not per-round. If the first tool already sent the definitive answer to the client, the AI's commentary in all following rounds is equally redundant. A new user message starts a fresh stream with `suppressOutput: false`.\n\n### stream_end and the spinner\n\nThe server sends a `stream_end` message after every completed stream (see [websocket-protocol.md](../hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md)). This is the client's signal to clear loading indicators.\n\nWithout `stream_end`, suppression would cause the tool spinner to hang forever. When the assistant response is suppressed, no `entry_upsert` arrives after the tool entry, so the tool stays as the last entry in the array. The client tracks an `isProcessing` state: `true` when the user sends a message, `false` when `stream_end` arrives. The spinner only shows when the tool is both last in the list *and* the stream is still processing.\n\n### MessageChunk union\n\nThe `MessageChunk` type gained two variants:\n\n<!-- doc-block: src/types/ai.ts#MessageChunk -->\n```typescript\ntype MessageChunk =\n  | {type: 'text'; text: string}\n```\n\nThese two variants are internal plumbing -- they never leave the server. The client only sees standard `OutgoingMessage` types over the WebSocket.\n\n## File Reference\n\n| File | What changed |\n|------|-------------|\n| `src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts` | `ToolResponse` interface, widened `ToolExecutor`, `normalizeToolResponse()` |\n| `src/types/ai.ts` | `tool_result` and `suppress_output` variants on `MessageChunk` |\n| `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` | `executeToolCalls` returns `ToolExecutionResult`, yields new chunks |\n| `src/transport/ws/messageHandler.ts` | `suppressOutput` on stream state, `processToolResultChunk`, guarded sends, `entry_skip`, blanking |","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"04a8c46b-6762-4538-bd40-c5c0680e961a","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:39:19.026Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"    const response = await toolRegistry.execute(tc.name, tc.input);\n    return {tc, response};\n  })\n);\n\n// Collect client messages from all tools\nconst clientMessages = responses.flatMap(({response}) => response.clientMessages ?? []);\n\n// Yield them as a chunk for the message handler\nif (clientMessages.length > 0) {\n  yield {type: 'tool_result', clientMessages};\n}\n\n// Signal suppression if any tool requested it\nif (responses.some(({response}) => response.suppressAssistantResponse)) {\n  yield {type: 'suppress_output'};\n}\n```\n\n### Message Handler\n\nTwo new chunk types flow through `processChunk`:\n\n- `tool_result` -- each client message in the array is serialized and sent directly to the WebSocket\n- `suppress_output` -- sets `state.suppressOutput = true` on the stream state\n\nWhen suppression is active, `streamSegment` and `commitAndClear` still call `appendEntry`, `appendDelta`, and `commitEntry` (so the session accumulates entries for context), but skip `sendUpsert`, `sendDelta`, and `sendCommit` (so the client sees nothing).\n\nWhen a new entry is created under suppression, the server sends an `entry_skip` message with the server-side index. Without this, server indices would diverge from the client's entry array, causing deltas and commits to target the wrong positions.\n\nIf the AI streamed text *before* the tool call (which happens when the model explains what it is about to do), that text was already sent to the client as an assistant entry. When suppression activates, the server retroactively blanks these entries by sending `entry_upsert` with empty content. The client already skips rendering empty assistant entries, so the pre-tool text disappears cleanly.\n\n**Suppression is sticky for the entire stream.** Once a tool sets `suppressAssistantResponse: true` in any round, all subsequent AI responses in that stream are suppressed -- even if later tool rounds do not set the flag. This is intentional: suppression is a per-message property, not per-round. If the first tool already sent the definitive answer to the client, the AI's commentary in all following rounds is equally redundant. A new user message starts a fresh stream with `suppressOutput: false`.\n\n### stream_end and the spinner\n\nThe server sends a `stream_end` message after every completed stream (see [websocket-protocol.md](../hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md)). The client only sees standard `OutgoingMessage` types over the WebSocket.\n\n## File Reference\n\n| File | What changed |\n|------|-------------|\n| `src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts` | `ToolResponse` interface, widened `ToolExecutor`, `normalizeToolResponse()` |\n| `src/types/ai.ts` | `tool_result` and `suppress_output` variants on `MessageChunk` |\n| `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` | `executeToolCalls` returns `ToolExecutionResult`, yields new chunks |\n| `src/transport/ws/messageHandler.ts` | `suppressOutput` on stream state, `processToolResultChunk`, guarded sends, `entry_skip`, blanking |","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01NwqCccgkdGFKr4Nc7uYVw5","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

(session: ChatSession, error: Error) => Promise<void>;\n37\t}\n38\t\n39\texport interface ChatOrchestrator {\n40\t  processMessage(session: ChatSession): Promise<string>;\n41\t = promptBuilder.build({...options?.promptBuilderOptions, toolInstructions});\n65\t  const tools = toolRegistry?.getDefinitions();\n66\t  const hooks = options?.hooks;\n67\t\n68\t  return {\n69\t    async processMessage(session: ChatSession): Promise<string> {\n70\t      return collectText(this.processMessageStream(session));\n71\t    },\n72\t\n73\t    async *processMessageStream(session: {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XpZoiSyfRB6V6ConWER1eJ","type":"tool_result","content":"# AGENTS.md\n\n## Context Loading Order\n\nAgents should read files in this order to understand the HAL Engine codebase:\n\n1. **README.md** - Overview, features, quick start, and project structure\n2. **package.json** - Dependencies, scripts, tech stack validation\n3. **tsconfig.json** - TypeScript configuration and compilation targets\n4. **src/index.ts** - Public API exports and module surface\n5. **src/config.ts** - Main factory function `createHalEngine()` and configuration schema\n6. **src/types/** - Core type definitions (ai.ts, session.ts, messages.ts, auth.ts, sessionStore.ts)\n7. **src/providers/** - AI provider implementations (bedrock, vertex, openai, anthropic, mock)\n8. **src/infrastructure/** - Support layers (builders, stores, parsers)\n9. **src/orchestration/** - Business logic (chatOrchestrator, tools, context management)\n10. **src/transport/** - Server setup (Express app, WebSocket, routes)\n11. **specs/** - Normative specs (architecture, WebSocket protocol, providers, tool responses)\n12. **adrs/** - Architecture Decision Records\n13. **docs/** - How-to guides, coding practices, and research spikes under docs/spikes/\n14. **.specify/spec.md** - Repo-level system spec (exempt from the spec header table convention)\n\n## Workflow Commands\n\n### Development\n```bash\nnpm run dev          # Start example/server.ts with hot reload (tsx watch)\nnpm run typecheck    # Run TypeScript type checking without emitting\n```\n\n### Building\n```bash\nnpm run build        # Compile TypeScript to dist/ (tsc)\nnpm start            # Run compiled JavaScript from dist/index.js\n```\n\n### Testing\n```bash\nnpm test             # Run Jest test suite\n# Tests use ts-jest for TypeScript compilation\n# Configuration: jest.config.js\n```\n\n### Code Quality\n```bash\nnpm run eslint       # Run ESLint with max-warnings 0 (zero-tolerance)\nnpm run prettier     # Format all src/**/*.ts with Prettier\nnpm run prettier:check  # Check formatting without writing\n```\n\n### All Checks (Recommended Pre-Commit)\n```bash\nnpm run verify\n```\n\nThat is the blocking set `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `verify` job runs on every\npull request, in the same order, and `scripts/verify-script.test.ts` fails if the\ntwo stop agreeing - so this command cannot quietly become a subset of what CI\ndemands. Two gates in that job are missing from it because both diff against\n`origin/main` and neither runs the same way on a checkout: the spec anchor check\nand the changelog check.\n\nTwo further CI jobs are worth running locally before a release or a change that\ntouches packaging: `npm run check:build-chain` (audit and action pins) and\n`npm run smoke` (packs the tarball and installs it outside this tree).\n\n## Spec Header Table\n\nEvery `specs/*/spec.md` opens with its title, then a two-column header table,\nand carries a lead paragraph of prose before its first `##` section:\n\n```markdown\n# <spec title>\n\n| Field  | Value                  |\n| ------ | ---------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#<n> |\n| Status | Draft                  |\n\n<lead paragraph>\n```\n\nThe lead paragraph does not have to sit immediately after the table: a spec\ncarrying a metadata block of its own may place that block between the two. What\nthe convention requires is that prose, rather than a section heading, follows\nthe header material, and that the prose runs to at least 40 characters -\nanything shorter is reported as no lead paragraph at all.\n\n`Issue` names the issue the spec was written against, or `n/a` for the specs\nthat predate this convention.\n\n`Status` is one of `Draft`, `In Progress` or `Shipped`. It is not a mood, and\nit is not a claim about how finished the feature is: it is the spec's own\ntest-citation coverage. No testable statement cited is `Draft`, some cited is\n`In Progress`, all cited is `Shipped`. A spec describing shipped, working\nbehaviour still reads `Draft` until its statements cite the tests that\nvalidate them.\n\nA spec that should no longer be graded takes a terminal value instead. The\nparser buckets roughly sixteen labels into five buckets, and the two terminal\nones skip the coverage check whatever the citation count:\n\n| Written into the spec                                            | Bucket                       |\n| ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |\n| `Draft`                                                          | `draft`                      |\n| `In Progress`, `In Review`, `Planning`, `WIP`, `Proposed`        | `in-progress`                |\n| `Shipped`, `Implemented`, `Complete`, `Accepted`, `Done`, `Live` | `shipped`                    |\n| `Retired`, `Superseded`, `Removed`, `Deprecated`, `Obsolete`     | `retired` - skips the check  |\n| `Rejected`, `Abandoned`                                          | `rejected` - skips the check |\n\nPrefer the canonical `Draft` / `In Progress` / `Shipped` for a live spec; reach\nfor `Retired` or `Rejected` rather than leaving a superseded spec to claim a\ncoverage tier it will never earn.\n\nADRs do not carry this table. They declare their state as YAML frontmatter\n`status:` instead - `accepted` for a decision the code implements, `proposed`\nfor one it does not. That field does mean maturity, which the spec `Status` row\nexplicitly does not. The difference is deliberate: ADRs are exempt from the\ncoverage tier, because asking a decision record to cite a test for every\nstatement it makes is the wrong ask of a decision record.\n\n`.specify/spec.md` is exempt from this section. It is the repo-level system\nspec, it predates the convention, and its content is deliberately frozen -\ngiving it a header table and a lead paragraph would mean writing new prose into\na file that is meant to stay unchanged. The exemption covers the header table\nand the lead paragraph only: the Lore coverage job does scan\n`.specify/spec.md`, so link placement still applies to it.\n\n## Spec Test Links\n\nStatements in `specs/*/spec.md` cite their validating test in a trailing\nparenthetical at the end of the statement, where a list item counts as one\nstatement and a paragraph counts per sentence. Overview, features, quick start, and project structure\n2. **package.json** - Dependencies, scripts, tech stack validation\n3. **tsconfig.json** - TypeScript configuration and compilation targets\n4. **src/index.ts** - Public API exports and module surface\n5. **src/config.ts** - Main factory function `createHalEngine()` and configuration schema\n6. **src/types/** - Core type definitions (ai.ts, session.ts, messages.ts, auth.ts, sessionStore.ts)\n7. **src/providers/** - AI provider implementations (bedrock, vertex, openai, anthropic, mock)\n8. **src/infrastructure/** - Support layers (builders, stores, parsers)\n9. **src/orchestration/** - Business logic (chatOrchestrator, tools, context management)\n10. **src/transport/** - Server setup (Express app, WebSocket, routes)\n11. **specs/** - Normative specs (architecture, WebSocket protocol, providers, tool responses)\n12. **adrs/** - Architecture Decision Records\n13. **docs/** - so this command cannot quietly become a subset of what CI\ndemands. Two gates in that job are missing from it because both diff against\n`origin/main` and neither runs the same way on a checkout: the spec anchor check\nand the changelog check.\n\nTwo further CI jobs are worth running locally before a release or a change that\ntouches packaging: `npm run check:build-chain` (audit and action pins) and\n`npm run smoke` (packs the tarball and installs it outside this tree).\n\n## Spec Header Table\n\nEvery `specs/*/spec.md` opens with its title, then a two-column header table,\nand carries a lead paragraph of prose before its first `##` section:\n\n```markdown\n# <spec title>\n\n| Field  | Value                  |\n| ------ | ---------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#<n> |\n| Status | Draft                  |\n\n<lead paragraph>\n```\n\nThe lead paragraph does not have to sit immediately after the table: a spec\ncarrying a metadata block of its own may place that block between the two. What\nthe convention requires is that prose, rather than a section heading, follows\nthe header material, and that the prose runs to at least 40 characters -\nanything shorter is reported as no lead paragraph at all.\n\n`Issue` names the issue the spec was written against, or `n/a` for the specs\nthat predate this convention.\n\n`Status` is one of `Draft`, `In Progress` or `Shipped`. It is not a mood, and\nit is not a claim about how finished the feature is: it is the spec's own\ntest-citation coverage. No testable statement cited is `Draft`, some cited is\n`In Progress`, all cited is `Shipped`. A spec describing shipped, working\nbehaviour still reads `Draft` until its statements cite the tests that\nvalidate them.\n\nA spec that should no longer be graded takes a terminal value instead. The\nparser buckets roughly sixteen labels into five buckets, and the two terminal\nones skip the coverage check whatever the citation count:\n\n| Written into the spec                                            | Bucket                       |\n| ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |\n| `Draft`                                                          | `draft`                      |\n| `In Progress`, `In Review`, `Planning`, `WIP`, `Proposed`        | `in-progress`                |\n| `Shipped`, `Implemented`, `Complete`, `Accepted`, `Done`, `Live` | `shipped`                    |\n| `Retired`, `Superseded`, `Removed`, `Deprecated`, `Obsolete`     | `retired` - skips the check  |\n| `Rejected`, `Abandoned`                                          | `rejected` - skips the check |\n\nPrefer the canonical `Draft` / `In Progress` / `Shipped` for a live spec; reach\nfor `Retired` or `Rejected` rather than leaving a superseded spec to claim a\ncoverage tier it will never earn.\n\nADRs do not carry this table. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014AP1B7JmUqaZ3DFjRTBhjj","type":"tool_result","content":"A citation counts as coverage only where it is trailing, and a test link placed\nanywhere else in the statement is a misplaced citation rather than a weaker one.\nUpstream `check:spec-links` reports it and exits 1, and the coverage job that\nconsumes these specs records nothing for it - so a spec can read as fully cited while its coverage is\nzero. A mid-statement link to something that is not a test file - a script, a\ndoc, another spec - is ordinary prose, is never reported, and was never coverage\nto begin with. A misplaced citation is fixed by moving it to the end of its\nstatement, never by deleting it.\n\nIn a test file the `#Lnn` anchor must land on the `it(`, `test(` or `describe(`\ndeclaration itself - not a blank line, not one holding only closing punctuation,\nand not a line inside the test body. A citation onto an `expect` renders and\nresolves like any other, and proves nothing: no reader can turn that line back\ninto the name of a case. `scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs` reports all four as\n`rotten` and exits 1 in both modes. A citation into a file that is not a test -\na workflow, a config, `package.json` - has no declaration to land on and is held\nonly to existing and carrying content.\n\nWhat no gate can catch is an anchor aimed at the wrong declaration: a real but\nunintended `it(` passes every check. The tell is that the test the statement\nmeant to cite goes on reading as uncited in the `require-spec-link` backlog.\n\nAnchors drift silently whenever a cited file gains or loses lines above them, so\nafter editing any cited file re-run `node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs`\nrather than hand-correcting the numbers. That script cannot repair a spec absent\nfrom the base ref, since it has no baseline to translate from; on the branch that\nintroduces a spec, its anchors have to be corrected by hand.\n\nThe convention's tooling is wired up here: `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n`node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs --check origin/main` all gate CI, and\n`re-lint/require-status-matches-coverage` derives each `Status` row from its\nspec's citations. `re-lint/require-spec-link` gates in `eslint.config.mjs`: every test cites a\nstatement, so an uncited new test fails the build. Its statement-side mirror,\n`require-statement-links`, runs at `warn` from `eslint.config.backlog.mjs` and\nreports without blocking - the severities the rule pair is designed for.\n\n## Commit Conventions\n\n### Format\nFollow conventional commits:\n```\n<type>(<scope>): <subject>\n\n<body>\n\n<footer>\n```\n\n### Types\n- `feat` - New feature (provider, tool, config option)\n- `fix` - Bug fix\n- `refactor` - Code restructuring without behavior change\n- `test` - Test additions or modifications\n- `docs` - Documentation only\n- `chore` - Build, deps, config, tooling\n- `perf` - Performance improvement\n\n### Scope\nOptional but recommended. Use domain areas:\n- `bedrock` / `vertex` / `openai` / `anthropic` / `mock` - Provider-specific\n- `tools` - Tool registry and execution\n- `orchestrator` - Chat orchestrator\n- `transport` - Express/WebSocket\n- `session` - Session management\n- `types` - Type definitions\n- `build` - Build/packaging\n\n### Examples\n```\nfeat(tools): add parallel tool execution batching\nfix(bedrock): handle streaming timeouts gracefully\nrefactor(orchestrator): extract context building logic\ndocs(providers): add Vertex AI implementation guide\ntest(tools): add registry conflict detection\n```\n\n### Rules\n- Use imperative mood: \"add\" not \"added\" or \"adds\"\n- Do not capitalize first letter of subject\n- No period (.) at end of subject\n- Limit subject to 50 characters\n- Reference issues in footer: `Fixes #123`, `Relates to #456`\n- Breaking changes in footer: `BREAKING CHANGE: description`\n\n## PR Requirements\n\n### Before Opening\n1. Run full check suite and ensure all pass:\n   ```bash\n   npm run verify\n   ```\n2. Create a feature branch from `main`: `git checkout -b feat/description`\n3. Commit with conventional format\n\n### PR Description Template\n```markdown\n## Description\nBrief summary of changes and rationale.\n\n## Type of Change\n- [ ] Feature","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"4e70fb23-fe25-4a93-8a07-9e7296edf2f3","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:39:36.702Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"A citation counts as coverage only where it is trailing, and a test link placed\nanywhere else in the statement is a misplaced citation rather than a weaker one.\nUpstream `check:spec-links` reports it and exits 1, and the coverage job that\nconsumes these specs records nothing for it - so a spec can read as fully cited while its coverage is\nzero. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HmEnQ3uFNmqtU7VZV1qVoB","type":"tool_result","content":"/** @type {import('ts-jest').JestConfigWithTsJest} */\nexport default {\n  testEnvironment: 'node',\n  roots: ['<rootDir>/src', '<rootDir>/scripts'],\n  testMatch: ['**/*.test.ts'],\n  // Fixture suites are inputs to the gate tests, not tests; one of them is deliberately skipped.\n  testPathIgnorePatterns: ['/node_modules/', '/scripts/fixtures/'],\n  extensionsToTreatAsEsm: ['.ts'],\n  moduleFileExtensions: ['ts', 'js', 'json'],\n  // NodeNext source writes `./x.js` for `./x.ts`; jest resolves the real file.\n  moduleNameMapper: {\n    '^(\\\\.{1,2}/.*)\\\\.js$': '$1',\n  },\n  transform: {\n    '^.+\\\\.tsx?$': ['ts-jest', {useESM: true}],\n  },\n  // src/ only: the scripts/ suites drive their subjects through spawnSync, so an\n  // instrumented .mjs would report zero regardless of how well it is tested.\n  collectCoverageFrom: ['src/**/*.ts', '!src/**/*.test.ts', '!src/providers/providerTestSupport.ts'],\n  // A ratchet, not a target: these are exactly what the suite measured when the\n  // gate landed, so any drop fails and any rise should move them up with it. A\n  // red run is fixed by adding the missing test, never by lowering the number\n  // (specs/hal-engine-npm-release/spec.md).\n  // The one exception: deleting covered code shrinks the denominator and lowers\n  // the percentage while the uncovered count is unchanged. Compare covered/total\n  // absolutes before re-baselining, and say so in the commit.\n  // Per-path floors set at measurement, exactly as the global row is. An earlier version set them at\n  // 70 with a comment claiming all four sat well above it - vertex's branches were 69.23, below it, and\n  // four groups gained an 18-to-30 point regression budget the single global floor had never allowed.\n  // `src/orchestration/tools/` is named separately because a path floor is a prefix match: without it a\n  // regression in the tools registry hides inside the orchestration figure, which is what these exist\n  // to prevent. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UstM8R1Gyuh6Ra9sf7M3uf","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Tool Responses\n2\t\n3\t| Field  | Value |\n4\t| ------ | ----- |\n5\t| Issue  | n/a   |\n6\t| Status | Draft |\n7\t\n8\tBy default, a tool executor returns a plain string that feeds back into the AI conversation. The client never sees the result -- it only sees the tool invocation (name and input parameters). This is fine for most tools, but sometimes a tool knows exactly what to show the user and does not need the AI to rephrase it.\n9\t\n10\tThe `ToolResponse` interface gives tools two additional capabilities:\n11\t\n12\t1. **Client messages** -- send one or more WebSocket messages directly to the client\n13\t2. **Suppress the AI response** -- keep the model's reply in conversation context but hide it from the client\n14\t\n15\tThese are opt-in. Tools that return a plain string continue to work exactly as before.\n16\t\n17\t## The ToolResponse Interface\n18\t\n19\t<!-- doc-block: src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts#ToolResponse -->\n20\t```typescript\n21\tinterface ToolResponse {\n22\t  result: string;\n23\t  clientMessages?: OutgoingMessage[];\n24\t  suppressAssistantResponse?: boolean;\n25\t}\n26\t```\n27\t\n28\t| Field | Purpose |\n29\t|-------|---------|\n30\t| `result` | The string the AI model sees -- identical to what a plain-string executor returns |"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"4b81182f-e3f4-42d9-b9f6-15a7d947f4e2","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:39:57.524Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/ff5ba54c6ad0/workspace/target/specs/hal-engine-tool-responses/spec.md","content":"# Tool Responses\n\n| Field  | Value |\n| ------ | ----- |\n| Issue  | n/a   |\n| Status | Draft |\n\nBy default, a tool executor returns a plain string that feeds back into the AI conversation. The client never sees the result -- it only sees the tool invocation (name and input parameters). This is fine for most tools, but sometimes a tool knows exactly what to show the user and does not need the AI to rephrase it.\n\nThe `ToolResponse` interface gives tools two additional capabilities:\n\n1. **Client messages** -- send one or more WebSocket messages directly to the client\n2. **Suppress the AI response** -- keep the model's reply in conversation context but hide it from the client\n\nThese are opt-in. Tools that return a plain string continue to work exactly as before.\n\n## The ToolResponse Interface\n\n<!-- doc-block: src/orchestration/tools/registry.ts#ToolResponse -->\n```typescript\ninterface ToolResponse {\n  result: string;\n  clientMessages?: OutgoingMessage[];\n  suppressAssistantResponse?: boolean;\n}\n```\n\n| Field | Purpose |\n|-------|---------|\n| `result` | The string the AI model sees -- identical to what a plain-string executor returns |","numLines":30,"startLine":1,"totalLines":219}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01UstM8R1Gyuh6Ra9sf7M3uf","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012LaDHzhH2JMM9g23DSQ7Qw","type":"tool_result","content":"import net from 'node:net';\nimport {createHalEngine} from './config.js';\nimport {log, setLogger} from './shared/logger.js';\nimport type {Logger} from './shared/logger.js';\nimport type {ChatSession} from './types/session.js';\nimport type {WsAuthenticator} from './types/auth.js';\n\n// Both options below are declared on HalEngineConfig and were dropped at the forwarding site.\n\nconst base = {\n  provider: {type: 'mock' as const},\n  prompt: {identity: 'test'},\n  auth: {ws: (async () => null) as unknown as WsAuthenticator},\n};\n\ndescribe('createHalEngine forwarding', () => {\n  it('forwards an orchestrator hook, so one passed through the config actually fires', async () => {\n    const seen: string[] = [];\n    const engine = createHalEngine({\n      ...base,\n      orchestrator: {hooks: {beforeSession: async () => void seen.push('beforeSession')}},\n    });\n    const session: ChatSession = {\n      sessionId: 's1',\n      userId: 'u1',\n      entries: [{role: 'user', content: 'hi', timestamp: ''}],\n    };\n\n    await engine.orchestrator.processMessage(session);\n\n    expect(seen).toEqual(['beforeSession']);\n  });\n\n  it('forwards transport.port, so the server listens where the config said', async () => {\n    const engine = createHalEngine({...base, transport: {port: 0}});\n\n    await engine.start();\n    const address = engine.server.address();\n    const port = typeof address === 'object' && address !== null ? {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01L3BiyTP3VtHoycc2a5TTDN","type":"tool_result","content":"/**\n * require-spec-link — every `it()`/`test()` must be linked to a spec or ADR.\n *\n * The spec-traceability convention authors the link INSIDE the spec.md/adr `.md`\n * as an inline trailing parenthetical — `Statement. ([validated by](test.ts#L42))`.\n * This rule inverts that view: it goes over each test declaration and fails the\n * ones that no spec or ADR statement references, so tests cannot silently drift\n * out of spec coverage.\n *\n * A test is linked when the spec/adr corpus carries a `([validated by](this#Lnn))`\n * link whose line falls inside the test's line span, or a whole-file link (no\n * `#L`) to the file. The corpus is read once per process (memoized per specsRoot)\n * via the shared parser — see `lib/spec-link-index.mjs`. Type info is off for\n * test files, so this works from the ESTree AST + filename + filesystem only.\n *\n * Detect-only: writing the link needs a human to pick which statement the test\n * validates, not a codemod.\n */\nimport path from \"node:path\";\nimport { isTestFile } from \"./lib/spec-parsers.mjs\";\nimport { buildLinkIndex, corpusExists, readSpecFiles, toPosix, } from \"./lib/spec-link-index.mjs\";\n/**\n * specsRoot+roots → index, so the corpus is walked once per process.\n *\n * Note: the cache is keyed on specsRoot only, with no mtime/content hash — a\n * single-pass `eslint`/CI run always reads fresh, but a long-lived ESLint\n * server (VS Code extension, --watch) will not see a spec link added after the\n * first lint until the server restarts.\n */\nconst indexCache = new Map();\n/** specsRoots we've already warned about having no spec/adr corpus, so a\n * misconfigured run warns once instead of once per linted test file.\n *\n * Scope is the whole Node process, matching `indexCache` above. In a one-shot\n * `eslint`/CI run that is exactly one warning. In a long-lived ESLint server\n * (VS Code, --watch) the module is not re-required between cycles, so once a\n * specsRoot has warned it stays quiet even after the CWD is fixed — the same\n * process-lifetime memoization caveat the corpus cache carries. Restart the\n * server (or the editor) to re-arm the warning. */\nconst warnedMissingCorpus = new Set();\nfunction getIndex(specsRoot, roots) {\n    // `\\0` separates every element so no root value can collide with the joiner.\n    const key = [specsRoot, ...roots].join(\"\\0\");\n    let index = indexCache.get(key);\n    if (!index) {\n        index = buildLinkIndex(readSpecFiles(specsRoot, roots));\n        indexCache.set(key, index);\n    }\n    return index;\n}\n/** The base identifier of a (possibly chained) callee — `it` in `it.each([])(…)`. */\nfunction rootCallName(callee) {\n    let node = callee;\n    while (node) {\n        if (node.type === \"Identifier\") {\n            return node.name;\n        }\n        if (node.type === \"MemberExpression\") {\n            node = node.object;\n            continue;\n        }\n        if (node.type === \"CallExpression\") {\n            node = node.callee;\n            continue;\n        }\n        // `it.each`table`(name, fn)` — the outer callee is a tagged template whose\n        // `.tag` is the `it.each` member chain; drill into it or the test escapes.\n        if (node.type === \"TaggedTemplateExpression\") {\n            node = node.tag;\n            continue;\n        }\n        return null;\n    }\n    return null;\n}\n/** A named test declaration: `it`/`test` (any modifier chain) with a string name. */\nfunction isNamedTestCall(node) {\n    const root = rootCallName(node.callee);\n    if (root !== \"it\" && root !== \"test\") {\n        return false;","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"fcb05664-b9b7-471b-a01b-b2c6413ea40f","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:40:18.077Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"/**\n * require-spec-link — every `it()`/`test()` must be linked to a spec or ADR.\n *\n * The spec-traceability convention authors the link INSIDE the spec.md/adr `.md`\n * as an inline trailing parenthetical — `Statement. Restart the\n * server (or the editor) to re-arm the warning. */\nconst warnedMissingCorpus = new Set();\nfunction getIndex(specsRoot, roots) {\n    // `\\0` separates every element so no root value can collide with the joiner.\n    const key = [specsRoot, ...roots].join(\"\\0\");\n    let index = indexCache.get(key);\n    if (!index) {\n        index = buildLinkIndex(readSpecFiles(specsRoot, roots));\n        indexCache.set(key, index);\n    }\n    return index;\n}\n/** The base identifier of a (possibly chained) callee — `it` in `it.each([])(…)`. */\nfunction rootCallName(callee) {\n    let node = callee;\n    while (node) {\n        if (node.type === \"Identifier\") {\n            return node.name;\n        }\n        if (node.type === \"MemberExpression\") {\n            node = node.object;\n            continue;\n        }\n        if (node.type === \"CallExpression\") {\n            node = node.callee;\n            continue;\n        }\n        // `it.each`table`(name, fn)` — the outer callee is a tagged template whose\n        // `.tag` is the `it.each` member chain; drill into it or the test escapes.\n        if (node.type === \"TaggedTemplateExpression\") {\n            node = node.tag;\n            continue;\n        }\n        return null;\n    }\n    return null;\n}\n/** A named test declaration: `it`/`test` (any modifier chain) with a string name. */\nfunction isNamedTestCall(node) {\n    const root = rootCallName(node.callee);\n    if (root !== \"it\" && root !== \"test\") {\n        return false;","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01L3BiyTP3VtHoycc2a5TTDN","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Qe1otF2C2zmTGKzrsLQtTz","type":"tool_result","content":"/**\n * test-imports-its-subject — a `*.test.ts` file must load the real thing it\n * tests: a first-party module, or (for a suite whose subject is an artifact\n * rather than code) the file itself, read from disk. Never a copy.\n *\n * Written after `memory-lifecycle.test.ts` was found importing only vitest and\n * then defining its own copy of the function it claimed to test, under the\n * comment \"(copied from memory-lifecycle.ts)\". Eleven scoring tests and three\n * parsing tests were green against re-implementations, with fourteen spec\n * anchors reporting those statements covered. A change to production code could\n * not have failed any of them.\n *\n * A `[validated by]` link proves a test EXISTS. This is the cheapest available\n * proof that the test also RUNS the thing.\n *\n * Deliberately not checked: whether the imported binding is actually called. A\n * test can legitimately import a type-adjacent helper and exercise the subject\n * indirectly, and chasing that needs type information this rule does not have.\n * The bar here is \"the real subject is loaded\", which is exactly the bar the\n * copy failed.\n *\n * First-party means a relative path, or a package under one of the npm scopes\n * named in `firstPartyScopes` (default `[]`, e.g. `[\"@re-cinq\"]` for a\n * monorepo whose workspace packages a suite may reach through a barrel).\n */\n/** Suites that drive a system rather than one module, so no subject is implied. */\nconst EXEMPT_SUFFIXES = [\n    \".integration.test\",\n    \".acceptance.test\",\n    \".e2e.test\",\n    \".contract.test\",\n];\n/** The same exemption spelled as a directory, which is how lore-api spells it. */\nconst EXEMPT_DIRS = [\"integration-tests\"];\n/** Accepts a scope with or without its trailing slash: `@org` and `@org/`. */\nfunction isUnderScope(source, scopes) {\n    return scopes.some((scope) => {\n        const prefix = scope.endsWith(\"/\") ? scope : `${scope}/`;\n        return source.startsWith(prefix);\n    });","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"36f7c2cd-f995-4072-923f-155366e43a32","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:40:50.188Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"/**\n * test-imports-its-subject — a `*.test.ts` file must load the real thing it\n * tests: a first-party module, or (for a suite whose subject is an artifact\n * rather than code) the file itself, read from disk. Never a copy.\n *\n * Written after `memory-lifecycle.test.ts` was found importing only vitest and\n * then defining its own copy of the function it claimed to test, under the\n * comment \"(copied from memory-lifecycle.ts)\". Eleven scoring tests and three\n * parsing tests were green against re-implementations, with fourteen spec\n * anchors reporting those statements covered. A change to production code could\n * not have failed any of them.\n *\n * A `[validated by]` link proves a test EXISTS. This is the cheapest available\n * proof that the test also RUNS the thing.\n *\n * Deliberately not checked: whether the imported binding is actually called. A\n * test can legitimately import a type-adjacent helper and exercise the subject\n * indirectly, and chasing that needs type information this rule does not have.\n * The bar here is \"the real subject is loaded\", which is exactly the bar the\n * copy failed.\n *\n * First-party means a relative path, or a package under one of the npm scopes\n * named in `firstPartyScopes` (default `[]`, e.g. `[\"@re-cinq\"]` for a\n * monorepo whose workspace packages a suite may reach through a barrel).\n */\n/** Suites that drive a system rather than one module, so no subject is implied. */\nconst EXEMPT_SUFFIXES = [\n    \".integration.test\",\n    \".acceptance.test\",\n    \".e2e.test\",\n    \".contract.test\",\n];\n/** The same exemption spelled as a directory, which is how lore-api spells it. */\nconst EXEMPT_DIRS = [\"integration-tests\"];\n/** Accepts a scope with or without its trailing slash: `@org` and `@org/`. */\nfunction isUnderScope(source, scopes) {\n    return scopes.some((scope) => {\n        const prefix = scope.endsWith(\"/\") ? scope : `${scope}/`;\n        return source.startsWith(prefix);\n    });","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01Qe1otF2C2zmTGKzrsLQtTz","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

ESLint can't move files, so this is\n * report-only; the fix is to relocate the file beside its source.\n */\nexport default {\n    meta: {\n        type: \"problem\",\n        docs: {\n            description: \"tests must be colocated with their source, not in __tests__/\",\n        },\n        schema: [],\n        messages: {\n            colocate: \"Test files must be colocated next to their source, not in a __tests__/ directory.\",\n        },\n    },\n    create(context) {\n        const inTestsDir = context.filename.split(/[/\\\\]/).includes(\"__tests__\");\n        if (!inTestsDir)\n            return {};\n        return {\n            Program(node) {\n                context.report({\n                    node,\n                    loc: { line: 1, column: 0 },\n                    messageId: \"colocate\",\n                });\n            },\n        };\n    },\n};","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"11442ec0-abd1-46a1-af16-60035eed511d","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:40:50.839Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"/**\n * require-colocated-tests — every test must live next to the source it covers,\n * never inside a `__tests__/` directory. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015vJmp6da1ccdp6v6s1poPV","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport type {Message, MessageChunk, ToolCall, UsageMetadata} from '../types/ai.js';\n2\timport type {ToolRegistry} from './tools/registry.js';\n3\t\n4\texport function shouldContinueToolLoop(\n5\t  stopReason: string,\n6\t  pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[],\n7\t  toolRegistry: ToolRegistry | undefined\n8\t): boolean {\n9\t  return stopReason === 'tool_use' && pendingToolCalls.length > 0 && toolRegistry !== undefined;\n10\t}\n11\t\n12\texport async function collectText(stream: AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk>): Promise<string> {\n13\t  let text = '';\n14\t  for await (const chunk of stream) {\n15\t    if (chunk.type === 'text') text += chunk.text;\n16\t  }\n17\t  return text;\n18\t}\n19\t\n20\texport function buildToolCallMessages(pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[]): Message {\n21\t  return {\n22\t    role: 'assistant',\n23\t    content: pendingToolCalls.map(tc => ({\n24\t      type: 'tool_use' as const,\n25\t      toolUseId: tc.id,\n26\t      name: tc.name,\n27\t      input: tc.input,\n28\t    })),\n29\t  };\n30\t}\n31\t\n32\texport function collectToolCalls(chunk: MessageChunk, pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[]): void {\n33\t  if (chunk.type === 'tool_use') pendingToolCalls.push(chunk.toolCall);\n34\t}\n35\t\n36\texport function extractStopReason(chunk: MessageChunk): string | null {\n37\t  return chunk.type === 'stop' ?
