{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01QFXnVivGznkbwJgX95GLTk","type":"tool_result","content":"180\t    const entry = session.entries[i];\n181\t    if (entry.role === 'user') return entry.content;\n182\t  }\n183\t  return '';\n184\t}\n185\t\n186\tfunction updateLastUserContent(session: ChatSession, content: string): void {\n187\t  for (let i = session.entries.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {\n188\t    const entry = session.entries[i];\n189\t    if (entry.role === 'user') {\n190\t      entry.content = content;\n191\t      return;\n192\t    }\n193\t  }\n194\t}\n195\t\n196\tasync function executeToolCalls(\n197\t  pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[],\n198\t  messages: Message[],\n199\t  toolRegistry: ToolRegistry,\n200\t  session: ChatSession\n201\t): Promise<ToolExecutionResult> {\n202\t  messages.push(buildToolCallMessages(pendingToolCalls));\n203\t\n204\t  const context: ToolContext = {\n205\t    userId: session.userId,\n206\t    sessionId: session.sessionId,\n207\t    workspaceId: session.workspaceId,\n208\t    authHeaders: session.authHeaders,\n209\t  };\n210\t\n211\t  const responses = await Promise.all(\n212\t    pendingToolCalls.map(async tc => {\n213\t      const response = await toolRegistry.execute(tc.name, tc.input, context);\n214\t      return {tc, response};\n215\t    })\n216\t  );\n217\t\n218\t  const toolResults: ToolResultContent[] = responses.map(({tc, response}) => ({\n219\t    type: 'tool_result' as const,\n220\t    toolUseId: tc.id,\n221\t    content: response.result,\n222\t  }));\n223\t  messages.push({role: 'user', content: toolResults});\n224\t\n225\t  const rawClientMessages = responses.flatMap(({response}) => response.clientMessages ?? []);\n226\t\n227\t  return {\n228\t    clientMessages: assignEntryIndices(rawClientMessages, session),\n229\t    suppressOutput: responses.some(({response}) => response.suppressAssistantResponse === true),\n230\t  };\n231\t}\n232\t\n233\tfunction assignEntryIndices(clientMessages: OutgoingMessage[], session: ChatSession): OutgoingMessage[] {\n234\t  return clientMessages.map(msg => {\n235\t    if (msg.type !== 'entry_upsert') return msg;\n236\t    const index = appendEntry(session, msg.entry);\n237\t    return {...msg, index};\n238\t  });\n239\t}\n240\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"fba30ff4-7b6a-4d0c-886d-59ec28153e7b","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:41:16.337Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/ff5ba54c6ad0/workspace/target/src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts","content":"    const entry = session.entries[i];\n    if (entry.role === 'user') return entry.content;\n  }\n  return '';\n}\n\nfunction updateLastUserContent(session: ChatSession, content: string): void {\n  for (let i = session.entries.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {\n    const entry = session.entries[i];\n    if (entry.role === 'user') {\n      entry.content = content;\n      return;\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nasync function executeToolCalls(\n  pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[],\n  messages: Message[],\n  toolRegistry: ToolRegistry,\n  session: ChatSession\n): Promise<ToolExecutionResult> {\n  messages.push(buildToolCallMessages(pendingToolCalls));\n\n  const context: ToolContext = {\n    userId: session.userId,\n    sessionId: session.sessionId,\n    workspaceId: session.workspaceId,\n    authHeaders: session.authHeaders,\n  };\n\n  const responses = await Promise.all(\n    pendingToolCalls.map(async tc => {\n      const response = await toolRegistry.execute(tc.name, tc.input, context);\n      return {tc, response};\n    })\n  );\n\n  const toolResults: ToolResultContent[] = responses.map(({tc, response}) => ({\n    type: 'tool_result' as const,\n    toolUseId: tc.id,\n    content: response.result,\n  }));\n  messages.push({role: 'user', content: toolResults});\n\n  const rawClientMessages = responses.flatMap(({response}) => response.clientMessages ?? []);\n\n  return {\n    clientMessages: assignEntryIndices(rawClientMessages, session),\n    suppressOutput: responses.some(({response}) => response.suppressAssistantResponse === true),\n  };\n}\n\nfunction assignEntryIndices(clientMessages: OutgoingMessage[], session: ChatSession): OutgoingMessage[] {\n  return clientMessages.map(msg => {\n    if (msg.type !== 'entry_upsert') return msg;\n    const index = appendEntry(session, msg.entry);\n    return {...msg, index};\n  });\n}\n","numLines":61,"startLine":180,"totalLines":240}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01QFXnVivGznkbwJgX95GLTk","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Pwi3of3ixo4idZxVbgb3gR","type":"tool_result","content":"/**\n * max-expects — one assertion per test. A test that checks five fields with\n * five `expect` calls stops at the first failure and hides the other four;\n * `expect(result).toEqual({...})` shows the whole diff at once, and a test\n * that genuinely needs two unrelated assertions is two tests.\n *\n * What counts: inside the callback of `it(...)` / `test(...)` and their\n * `.only` / `.skip` / `.each(...)` forms, every `expect(...)` call — the\n * ROOT call, so `expect(x).resolves.toEqual(y)` is one, not three.\n * `expect.assertions(n)` and `expect.hasAssertions()` are bookkeeping, not\n * assertions, and are not counted. Helper functions declared inside the test\n * body count toward the test that contains them: hiding assertions in a local\n * closure does not make the test smaller.\n *\n * Reported at the test call. Detect-only: which assertions merge into one\n * object and which become their own test is the author's call.\n */\nconst TEST_ROOTS = new Set([\"it\", \"test\"]);\nconst TEST_MODIFIERS = new Set([\"only\", \"skip\", \"each\", \"concurrent\"]);\nfunction isTestRoot(node) {\n    return node.type === \"Identifier\" && TEST_ROOTS.has(node.name);\n}\nfunction isTestModifier(node) {\n    return (node.type === \"MemberExpression\" &&\n        !node.computed &&\n        isTestRoot(node.object) &&\n        TEST_MODIFIERS.has(node.property.name));\n}\nfunction isTestCall(node) {\n    const { callee } = node;\n    if (isTestRoot(callee) || isTestModifier(callee)) {\n        return true;\n    }\n    // it.each(table)(\"name\", fn) — the callee is itself the `it.each(...)` call\n    return callee.type === \"CallExpression\" && isTestModifier(callee.callee);\n}\nfunction isExpectRootCall(node) {\n    return node.callee.type === \"Identifier\" && node.callee.name === \"expect\";\n}\nexport default {","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"e0622fc5-78ce-4c45-9043-668e88c93461","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:41:55.873Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"/**\n * max-expects — one assertion per test. A test that checks five fields with\n * five `expect` calls stops at the first failure and hides the other four;\n * `expect(result).toEqual({...})` shows the whole diff at once, and a test\n * that genuinely needs two unrelated assertions is two tests.\n *\n * What counts: inside the callback of `it(...)` / `test(...)` and their\n * `.only` / `.skip` / `.each(...)` forms, every `expect(...)` call — the\n * ROOT call, so `expect(x).resolves.toEqual(y)` is one, not three.\n * `expect.assertions(n)` and `expect.hasAssertions()` are bookkeeping, not\n * assertions, and are not counted. Helper functions declared inside the test\n * body count toward the test that contains them: hiding assertions in a local\n * closure does not make the test smaller.\n *\n * Reported at the test call. Detect-only: which assertions merge into one\n * object and which become their own test is the author's call.\n */\nconst TEST_ROOTS = new Set([\"it\", \"test\"]);\nconst TEST_MODIFIERS = new Set([\"only\", \"skip\", \"each\", \"concurrent\"]);\nfunction isTestRoot(node) {\n    return node.type === \"Identifier\" && TEST_ROOTS.has(node.name);\n}\nfunction isTestModifier(node) {\n    return (node.type === \"MemberExpression\" &&\n        !node.computed &&\n        isTestRoot(node.object) &&\n        TEST_MODIFIERS.has(node.property.name));\n}\nfunction isTestCall(node) {\n    const { callee } = node;\n    if (isTestRoot(callee) || isTestModifier(callee)) {\n        return true;\n    }\n    // it.each(table)(\"name\", fn) — the callee is itself the `it.each(...)` call\n    return callee.type === \"CallExpression\" && isTestModifier(callee.callee);\n}\nfunction isExpectRootCall(node) {\n    return node.callee.type === \"Identifier\" && node.callee.name === \"expect\";\n}\nexport default {","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01Pwi3of3ixo4idZxVbgb3gR","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LMvC3ovVxBZnD3h6T5cMCW","type":"tool_result","content":"/**\n * no-nondeterministic-tests — a test must produce the same result on every\n * run. A clock read or a random draw inside a test file makes its outcome\n * depend on when and where it ran, which is how \"flaky\" gets into the\n * vocabulary.\n *\n * Active only in test files (`*.test.*` / `*.spec.*`). What counts:\n *   - `Math.random()`                        — tamed by `vi.spyOn(Math, \"random\")` / `jest.spyOn(Math, \"random\")`\n *   - `Date.now()`, `new Date()` (no args),\n *     `performance.now()`                    — tamed by `vi|jest.useFakeTimers()` / `vi|jest.setSystemTime()`\n *   - `crypto.randomUUID()`                  — tamed by `vi|jest.mock(\"crypto\")` / `(\"node:crypto\")`\n * A taming call anywhere in the file (any `beforeEach`, any test) exempts\n * its whole category: the visitor collects offenders and taming markers in\n * one pass and decides at `Program:exit`, so order within the file does not\n * matter. `new Date(\"2026-01-01\")` is a fixed value and is never reported.\n *\n * Detect-only: the fix is to inject the clock/random source or fake it.\n */\nconst TEST_FILE = /\\.(test|spec)\\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/;\nconst TEST_FRAMEWORKS = new Set([\"vi\", \"jest\"]);\nconst CRYPTO_MODULE = /^(node:)?crypto$/;\nconst OFFENDER_CATEGORY = {\n    \"Math.random\": \"random\",\n    \"Date.now\": \"time\",\n    \"performance.now\": \"time\",\n    \"crypto.randomUUID\": \"uuid\",\n};\nfunction isIdentifierPair(callee) {\n    return (callee.object.type === \"Identifier\" && callee.property.type === \"Identifier\");\n}\nfunction memberCallName(node) {\n    const { callee } = node;\n    const isPlainMember = callee.type === \"MemberExpression\" && !callee.computed;\n    if (!isPlainMember || !isIdentifierPair(callee)) {\n        return null;\n    }\n    return { object: callee.object.name, method: callee.property.name };\n}\nfunction isMathRandomSpy(node) {\n    const [target, method] = node.arguments;\n    return (target?.type === \"Identifier\" &&\n        target.name === \"Math\" &&\n        method?.type === \"Literal\" &&\n        method.value === \"random\");\n}\nfunction isCryptoMock(node) {\n    const [moduleName] = node.arguments;\n    return (moduleName?.type === \"Literal\" &&\n        CRYPTO_MODULE.test(String(moduleName.value)));\n}","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"5f576353-8d1e-4a2d-b7e7-e1af627cc06d","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:42:15.429Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"/**\n * no-nondeterministic-tests — a test must produce the same result on every\n * run. A clock read or a random draw inside a test file makes its outcome\n * depend on when and where it ran, which is how \"flaky\" gets into the\n * vocabulary.\n *\n * Active only in test files (`*.test.*` / `*.spec.*`). What counts:\n *   - `Math.random()`                        — tamed by `vi.spyOn(Math, \"random\")` / `jest.spyOn(Math, \"random\")`\n *   - `Date.now()`, `new Date()` (no args),\n *     `performance.now()`                    — tamed by `vi|jest.useFakeTimers()` / `vi|jest.setSystemTime()`\n *   - `crypto.randomUUID()`                  — tamed by `vi|jest.mock(\"crypto\")` / `(\"node:crypto\")`\n * A taming call anywhere in the file (any `beforeEach`, any test) exempts\n * its whole category: the visitor collects offenders and taming markers in\n * one pass and decides at `Program:exit`, so order within the file does not\n * matter. `new Date(\"2026-01-01\")` is a fixed value and is never reported.\n *\n * Detect-only: the fix is to inject the clock/random source or fake it.\n */\nconst TEST_FILE = /\\.(test|spec)\\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/;\nconst TEST_FRAMEWORKS = new Set([\"vi\", \"jest\"]);\nconst CRYPTO_MODULE = /^(node:)?crypto$/;\nconst OFFENDER_CATEGORY = {\n    \"Math.random\": \"random\",\n    \"Date.now\": \"time\",\n    \"performance.now\": \"time\",\n    \"crypto.randomUUID\": \"uuid\",\n};\nfunction isIdentifierPair(callee) {\n    return (callee.object.type === \"Identifier\" && callee.property.type === \"Identifier\");\n}\nfunction memberCallName(node) {\n    const { callee } = node;\n    const isPlainMember = callee.type === \"MemberExpression\" && !callee.computed;\n    if (!isPlainMember || !isIdentifierPair(callee)) {\n        return null;\n    }\n    return { object: callee.object.name, method: callee.property.name };\n}\nfunction isMathRandomSpy(node) {\n    const [target, method] = node.arguments;\n    return (target?.type === \"Identifier\" &&\n        target.name === \"Math\" &&\n        method?.type === \"Literal\" &&\n        method.value === \"random\");\n}\nfunction isCryptoMock(node) {\n    const [moduleName] = node.arguments;\n    return (moduleName?.type === \"Literal\" &&\n        CRYPTO_MODULE.test(String(moduleName.value)));\n}","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01LMvC3ovVxBZnD3h6T5cMCW","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01QKocKx1u4cC8NU5N7wfgfx","type":"tool_result","content":"spec.md\n# Config-Installed Orchestrator Hooks\n\n| Field  | Value                |\n| ------ | -------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#36 |\n| Status | Shipped              |\n\n`OrchestratorHooks` is the engine's only lifecycle seam, and `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` is the supported way to install it: `createHalEngine` forwards the set to `createChatOrchestrator`, so a hook a deployer passes through the factory reaches the running orchestrator rather than sitting unread on the config object. This spec pins that forwarding and the measured semantics the seam already carries, so the hook-based features that follow in this epic — transparency disclosure, audit and usage recording, human oversight, provider failover — specify against real behaviour rather than against what the hook names suggest.\n\n## Installing hooks through config\n\n- A hook passed through `orchestrator.hooks` is forwarded to the orchestrator and fires ([validated by: forwards an orchestrator hook, so one passed through the config actually fires](../../src/config.test.ts#L17)).\n- An engine built with no `orchestrator` key at all still processes a message, so the field is optional in fact and not only in the type ([validated by: processes a message when the config declares no orchestrator key at all](../../src/config.test.ts#L93)).\n- A `beforeModelResponse` hook installed through config is handed the base system prompt built from `prompt`, proving the hook reaches the orchestrator and is wired into the prompt pipeline rather than only being stored on the config ([validated by: hands a config-installed beforeModelResponse hook the built base system prompt, not merely storing it](../../src/config.test.ts#L106)).\n- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L194)).\n- For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L130)).\n\n## Engine startup\n\n- The engine's `start()` method rejects the returned promise when it cannot bind the configured port, rather than emitting an unhandled error event that would take the process down ([validated by: rejects instead of taking the process down with an unhandled error event](../../src/config.test.ts#L172)).\n\n## Measured semantics — out of scope\n\nThese three properties of `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` are recorded so the hook features that follow specify against real behaviour. This issue changes none of them; each is a separate, out-of-scope change owned by no issue in this epic.\n\n- `beforeSession` is awaited before the `try` block opens ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L74](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L74)), so a throw there escapes before the `catch`/`finally` exist and skips both `onError` and `afterSession`.\n- The `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error` ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L127](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L127)), so a rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string or object — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.\n- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L125](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L125)), and is passed the `lastUsage` local — the most recent round's usage, not a sum across tool rounds, so a deployer using the hook for billing or audit under-counts every turn that ran tools.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"c09eaf9e-f79c-44e0-93d3-dd23d5187586","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:43:04.873Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"spec.md\n# Config-Installed Orchestrator Hooks\n\n| Field  | Value                |\n| ------ | -------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#36 |\n| Status | Shipped              |\n\n`OrchestratorHooks` is the engine's only lifecycle seam, and `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` is the supported way to install it: `createHalEngine` forwards the set to `createChatOrchestrator`, so a hook a deployer passes through the factory reaches the running orchestrator rather than sitting unread on the config object. This spec pins that forwarding and the measured semantics the seam already carries, so the hook-based features that follow in this epic — transparency disclosure, audit and usage recording, human oversight, provider failover — specify against real behaviour rather than against what the hook names suggest.\n\n## Installing hooks through config\n\n- A hook passed through `orchestrator.hooks` is forwarded to the orchestrator and fires ([validated by: forwards an orchestrator hook, so one passed through the config actually fires](../../src/config.test.ts#L17)).\n- An engine built with no `orchestrator` key at all still processes a message, so the field is optional in fact and not only in the type ([validated by: processes a message when the config declares no orchestrator key at all](../../src/config.test.ts#L93)).\n- A `beforeModelResponse` hook installed through config is handed the base system prompt built from `prompt`, proving the hook reaches the orchestrator and is wired into the prompt pipeline rather than only being stored on the config ([validated by: hands a config-installed beforeModelResponse hook the built base system prompt, not merely storing it](../../src/config.test.ts#L106)).\n- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L194)).\n- For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L130)).\n\n## Engine startup\n\n- The engine's `start()` method rejects the returned promise when it cannot bind the configured port, rather than emitting an unhandled error event that would take the process down ([validated by: rejects instead of taking the process down with an unhandled error event](../../src/config.test.ts#L172)).\n\n## Measured semantics — out of scope\n\nThese three properties of `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` are recorded so the hook features that follow specify against real behaviour. This issue changes none of them; each is a separate, out-of-scope change owned by no issue in this epic.\n\n- `beforeSession` is awaited before the `try` block opens ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L74](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L74)), so a throw there escapes before the `catch`/`finally` exist and skips both `onError` and `afterSession`.\n- The `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error` ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L127](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L127)), so a rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string or object — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.\n- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L125](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L125)), and is passed the `lastUsage` local — the most recent round's usage, not a sum across tool rounds, so a deployer using the hook for billing or audit under-counts every turn that ran tools.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01QKocKx1u4cC8NU5N7wfgfx","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-6","id":"msg_011CgmrND4Lsjufi3Y9davTb","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01QpqiQqLfQWWpMSAwMQW7XJ","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/ff5ba54c6ad0/workspace/target/src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts","content":"import {ToolRegistry} from './registry.js';\nimport {createChatOrchestrator} from '../chatOrchestrator.js';\nimport {PromptBuilder} from '../../infrastructure/builders/promptBuilder.js';\nimport type {AIProvider, MessageChunk, SendMessageParams} from '../../types/ai.js';\n\ndescribe('ToolRegistry input schema validation', () => {\n  it('rejects a call missing a required field and never invokes the executor', async () => {\n    let callCount = 0;\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    registry.register(\n      {\n        name: 'weather',\n        description: 'get weather',\n        inputSchema: {type: 'object', properties: {location: {type: 'string'}}, required: ['location']},\n      },\n      async () => {\n        callCount++;\n        return 'sunny';\n      }\n    );\n\n    const response = await registry.execute('weather', {});\n\n    expect({callCount, mentionsLocation: response.result.includes('location')}).toEqual({\n      callCount: 0,\n      mentionsLocation: true,\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('a schema-invalid tool call does not throw so the model receives the rejection text and can retry', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    registry.register(\n      {\n        name: 'weather',\n        description: 'get weather',\n        inputSchema: {type: 'object', properties: {location: {type: 'string'}}, required: ['location']},\n      },\n      async () => 'sunny'\n    );\n\n    const capturedMessages: SendMessageParams[] = [];\n    let round = 0;\n    const provider: AIProvider = {\n      async *sendMessage(params: SendMessageParams): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n        capturedMessages.push(params);\n        round++;\n        if (round === 1) {\n          yield {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'call-1', name: 'weather', input: {}}};\n          yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'};\n        } else {\n          yield {type: 'text', text: 'retry done'};\n          yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'};\n        }\n      },\n      async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n        return {} as T;\n      },\n    };\n\n    const promptBuilder = new PromptBuilder({identity: 'test'});\n    const orchestrator = createChatOrchestrator(provider, promptBuilder, registry);\n    const session = {\n      sessionId: 's1',\n      userId: 'u1',\n      entries: [{role: 'user' as const, content: 'what is the weather?', timestamp: ''}],\n    };\n\n    const result = await orchestrator.processMessage(session);\n    const round2Input = JSON.stringify(capturedMessages.at(1)?.messages ?? []);\n\n    expect({result, rejectionFedBack: round2Input.includes('location')}).toEqual({\n      result: 'retry done',\n      rejectionFedBack: true,\n    });\n  });\n});\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1570,"cache_read_input_tokens":74722,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1570},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"5f34d47c-b497-4067-b7ad-404e379d2d51","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:43:30.354Z","request_id":"req_011CgmrNCYqB25EadUoDEori"}

executor', async () => {\n    let callCount = 0;\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    registry.register(\n      {\n        name: 'weather',\n        description: 'get weather',\n        inputSchema: {type: 'object', properties: {location: {type: 'string'}}, required: ['location']},\n      },\n      async () => {\n        callCount++;\n        return 'sunny';\n      }\n    );\n\n    const response = await registry.execute('weather', {});\n\n    expect({callCount, mentionsLocation: response.result.includes('location')}).toEqual({\n      callCount: 0,\n      mentionsLocation: true,\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('a schema-invalid tool call does not throw so the model receives the rejection text and can retry', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    registry.register(\n      {\n        name: 'weather',\n        description: 'get weather',\n        inputSchema: {type: 'object', properties: {location: {type: 'string'}}, required: ['location']},\n      },\n      async () => 'sunny'\n    );\n\n    const capturedMessages: SendMessageParams[] = [];\n    let round = 0;\n    const provider: AIProvider = {\n      async *sendMessage(params: SendMessageParams): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n        capturedMessages.push(params);\n        round++;\n        if (round === 1) {\n          yield {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: 'call-1', name: 'weather', input: {}}};\n          yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use'};\n        } else {\n          yield {type: 'text', text: 'retry done'};\n          yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn'};\n        }\n      },\n      async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n        return {} as T;\n      },\n    };\n\n    const promptBuilder = new PromptBuilder({identity: 'test'});\n    const orchestrator = createChatOrchestrator(provider, promptBuilder, registry);\n    const session = {\n      sessionId: 's1',\n      userId: 'u1',\n      entries: [{role: 'user' as const, content: 'what is the weather?', timestamp: ''}],\n    };\n\n    const result = await orchestrator.processMessage(session);\n    const round2Input = JSON.stringify(capturedMessages.at(1)?.messages ?? []);\n\n    expect({result, rejectionFedBack: round2Input.includes('location')}).toEqual({\n      result: 'retry done',\n      rejectionFedBack: true,\n    });\n  });\n});\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01QpqiQqLfQWWpMSAwMQW7XJ","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-6","id":"msg_011CgmrPcT3VrA1dJYho8TB7","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01RFHZ78iuzjbMEsHYDnmgPv","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/ff5ba54c6ad0/workspace/target/specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md","content":"# Tool Input Validation\n\n| Field  | Value                |\n| ------ | -------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#37 |\n| Status | Draft                |\n\n`ToolRegistry.execute` currently calls the executor without checking the input it received against the tool's declared `inputSchema`. Every tool declares a JSON Schema that is forwarded verbatim to the AI providers, but nothing validates what comes back from the model. The registry pushes this check onto each executor — differently each time, or not at all. This spec defines the validation contract so a malformed call fails predictably at the registry layer, once, and in a form the model can read and correct.\n\n## Validation on execute\n\n- When `execute` is called with input that fails the tool's declared `inputSchema` (for example, a missing required field), it returns a `ToolResponse` whose `result` names the failing constraint — it does not call the executor, so a tool that relies on seeing malformed input to produce its own error message stops seeing it ([validated by: rejects a call missing a required field and never invokes the executor](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L7)).\n\n- Because `execute` returns a `ToolResponse` on schema failure rather than throwing, the rejection text is forwarded to the model as a tool result in the next round's messages, giving the model the information it needs to issue a corrected call; the conversation does not end with a transport error ([validated by: a schema-invalid tool call does not throw so the model receives the rejection text and can retry](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L30)).\n\n## Out of scope\n\nExtra properties in valid input pass through to the executor unchanged — no coercion, no defaults, no stripping of additional properties. The `Unknown tool: <name>` throw for an unregistered name is unchanged. Output validation, hallucination detection, per-tool authorisation, and retry/timeout policy are out of scope.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":489,"cache_read_input_tokens":77678,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":489},"output_tokens":24,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"73e1c788-2d89-4c54-b098-f9a5eb167e3b","uuid":"98403b78-bdcc-4c4a-9d4d-08db0e3ebd29","timestamp":"2026-11-06T14:43:53.774Z","request_id":"req_011CgmrPbrp3rBL53xQYuHr9"}
